&=\det(\mathbf{d}(\omega))^{1/2}\cdot\widetilde{g}(\mathbf{d}(\omega)\langle\cdot-y\rangle),\text{ with }\widetilde{g}:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\bigl(\theta\circ\textbf{log}\bigr).\end{split}

Thus,
\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi)
is a wavelet system in the sense
of
[
15
,
47
]
, with the dilation group
given by the diagonal
d\times d
-matrices with entries in
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
The derivations above do not seem to generalize, however, to a setting that recovers
wavelets with respect to general dilation groups.
Finally, the expression of
g_{y,\omega}
through linear operators applied to
a single
mother wavelet

\widetilde{g}
defined in the time-domain relies on
properties of the coordinate-wise logarithm
log
and does not generalize to arbitrary warping functions
\Phi
.

Radial warping.

By choosing the warping function
\Phi
to be radial,
we can construct time-frequency systems with frequency resolution depending
on the modulus
|\xi|
of
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. The deformation is then fixed on any

(d-1)
-sphere of fixed radius, similar to isotropic wavelets
(see
[
32
, Section 2.6]
and
[
48
, Example 2.30]
).
Generally, radial warping functions are of the form

\Phi_{\varrho}:\quad\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad\xi\mapsto\varrho(|\xi|)\cdot\xi/|\xi|,

for a strictly increasing diffeomorphism
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
.
Under suitable additional assumptions on
\varrho
,
it can then be shown that
(\Phi_{\varrho})^{-1}=\Phi_{\varrho^{-1}}

and that
\Phi_{\varrho}
is a warping function as per Definition
3.1
.
It will be shown in future work that radial warping does
not

recover isotropic wavelets exactly in dimensions
d>1
,
for any choice of
\varrho
, but that warped time-frequency systems can be close to isotropic wavelets
in a sense that will be made formal in the mentioned follow-up work.
An in depth study of radial warping with some specific examples is provided in Section
8
.

An explicit, exotic example for
d=2
.

To demonstrate that there is potential for warping functions
beyond the separable and radial cases, consider the
continuous
\mathcal{C}^{1}
-diffeomorphism

\Phi:\quad\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{2},\quad\xi\mapsto\big(e^{\xi_{2}}\,\xi_{1},\,\,\xi_{2}\big)^{T}.

It is straightforward to see that
\Phi
is a diffeomorphism with inverse

\Phi^{-1}(\tau)=(e^{-\tau_{2}}\,\tau_{1},\,\,\tau_{2})^{T}
, which satisfies

\mathrm{D}\Phi^{-1}(\tau)=\begin{pmatrix}e^{-\tau_{2}}&-e^{-\tau_{2}}\,\tau_{1}\\
0&1\\
\end{pmatrix}

and hence
w(\tau)=\det(\mathrm{D}\Phi^{-1}(\tau))=e^{-\tau_{2}}>0
.
Moreover, it is easy to see that
w
is multiplicative (and in particular submultiplicative)
and hence self-moderate.
Thus,
\Phi
is a valid warping function that is neither separable nor radial.

4
Membership of the reproducing kernel in
\mathcal{B}_{m}

As we saw in Section
2.4
(see in particular Assumption
2.11
),
the main challenge in verifying the applicability of coorbit theory
for a continuous Parseval frame
\Psi
lies in showing that (the maximal function of)
the reproducing kernel
K_{\Psi}
is contained
\mathcal{A}_{m_{v}}
or
\mathcal{B}_{m_{0}}
,
for suitable weights
m_{v},m_{0}:\Lambda\times\Lambda\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
We will do so in two steps:
(1) In the present section, we will derive verifiable conditions on the warping function
\Phi

and the prototype function
\theta
which ensure that the warped time-frequency system

\Psi=\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi)
satisfies
K_{\Psi}\in\mathcal{B}_{m}
,
for a weight
m
satisfying suitable assumptions.
(2) In Section
6
, we do the same for the
\Gamma
-oscillation
of
\Psi
and additionally demonstrate that
\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}
can be made arbitrarily small
by choosing an appropriate covering
\mathcal{V}
.
Then, the desired properties of the maximal kernel
\textrm{M}_{\mathcal{V}}K_{\Psi}

are a consequence of Remark
2.20
.

To prepare for the treatment of the
\Gamma
-oscillation,
we already consider mixed kernels in the present section.
This setting only requires little additional effort.
We begin by introducing some notation and conditions that will be used throughout this section.

Notation & Definition 4.1
.

By
\Phi
, we denote a warping function
\Phi:D\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
with associated weights
w,w_{0}
as in
Definition

3.1
,

A=\mathrm{D}\Phi^{-1}
and
\Lambda:=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times D
.
In all instances,
\theta,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\mathbf{L}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and we denote the mixed kernel associated with
\mathcal{G}(\theta_{1},\Phi)
and
\mathcal{G}(\theta_{2},\Phi)

by
K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}:=K_{\mathcal{G}(\theta_{1},\Phi),\mathcal{G}(\theta_{2},\Phi)}
.
Finally, for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, we write

\mathcal{G}(\theta_{\ell},\Phi)=\bigl(g_{y,\omega}^{[\ell]}\bigr)_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\omega\in D}=\bigl(\mathbf{T}_{y}\,\widecheck{g_{\omega}^{[\ell]}}\bigr)_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\omega\in D}.

1.

If there is a continuous function
m^{\Phi}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}

satisfying

m\big((x,\Phi^{-1}(\sigma)),(y,\Phi^{-1}(\tau))\big)\leq m^{\Phi}(x-y,\sigma-\tau)\qquad\forall\,x,y,\sigma,\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(4.1)

then we say that
m
is
\Phi
-convolution-dominated
(by
m^{\Phi}
).
If that is the case, we denote by
M:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
the weight

M(x,\tau):=\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d},|y|\leq R_{\Phi}|x|}\big[\sqrt{w_{0}(\tau)}\,m^{\Phi}(y,\tau)\big]\quad\text{where}\quad R_{\Phi}:=\sup_{\xi\in D}\|\mathrm{D}\Phi(\xi)\|\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\cup\{\infty\}.

(4.2)

2.

If there exists an
m^{\Phi}
as in (
1
),
such that
m
is
\Phi
-convolution-dominated by
m^{\Phi}
and

R_{\Phi}<\infty\qquad\text{or}\qquad m^{\Phi}(x,\sigma)\lesssim m^{\Phi}(0,\sigma)\text{ for all }x,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(4.3)

then we say that
m
is
\Phi
-compatible
(with dominating weight
m^{\Phi}
).

Furthermore, we require a slightly stricter and more structured notion
of regularity for warping functions.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
\varnothing\neq D\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be an open set and fix an integer
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
A map
\Phi:D\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a
k
-admissible warping function

with
control weight

v_{0}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
if
v_{0}
is continuous, submultiplicative and radially increasing
and
\Phi
satisfies the following assumptions:

•

\Phi
is a
C^{k+1}
-diffeomorphism.

•

A=\mathrm{D}\Phi^{-1}
has positive determinant.

•

With

\phi_{\tau}\left({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\right):=\left(A^{-1}(\tau)A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\right)^{T}=A^{T}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\cdot A^{-T}(\tau),

(4.4)

we have

\left\|\partial^{\alpha}\phi_{\tau}\left({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\right)\right\|\leq v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\qquad\text{ for all }\tau,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ and all multiindices }\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d},~\left|\alpha\right|\leq k.

(4.5)

Remark 4.3
.

1) The function
\phi_{\tau}
describes the regularity of
A
around
\tau
;
its relevance will become clear before long,
see Equation (
4.27
) below.

2) On the right-hand side of (
4.5
),
one could allow constants
C_{\alpha}
and different weights
\tilde{v}_{\alpha}

not necessarily being radially increasing, therefore obtaining tighter bounds on

\left\|\partial^{\alpha}\phi_{\tau}\left({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\right)\right\|
.
However, whenever such
C_{\alpha}
,
\tilde{v}_{\alpha}
exist, there also exists
a weight
v_{0}
satisfying all the requirements
of Definition
4.2
.

3) We remark that (
4.5
) generalizes the conditions
mentioned in
[
61
]
, even for the case
d=1
considered there.

Theorem

4.4
below shows that smoothness of the prototypes
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}

and decay (or localization) of their partial derivatives implies
K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\!\in\!\mathcal{B}_{m}
,
provided that
m
is
\Phi
-compatible.
In particular, all conditions are surely satisfied
for arbitrary
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
The proof of
Theorem

4.4
is deferred to the end of the section.

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
\Phi
be a
(d+p+1)
-admissible warping function
with control weight
v_{0}
, where
p=0
if
R_{\Phi}=\infty
, defined as in (
4.2
), and
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
otherwise.
Let furthermore
m:\Lambda\times\Lambda\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a symmetric weight satisfying

m\bigl((y,\xi),(z,\eta)\bigr)\leq(1+|y-z|)^{p}\cdot v_{1}\bigl(\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\eta)\bigr),\text{ for all }y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ and }\xi,\eta\in D,

(4.6)

for some continuous and submultiplicative weight
v_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}

satisfying
v_{1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=v_{1}(-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})
for all
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Finally, with

w_{2}:\quad\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+},\quad{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\mapsto(1+|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}|)^{d+1}\cdot v_{1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot[v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{9d/2+3p+3},

assume that
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{d+p+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{n}}\theta_{\ell}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{w_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad\text{ for all }j\in\underline{d},\ \ell\in\{1,2\},\ 0\leq n\leq d+p+1\,,

and let

C_{\max}:=C_{\max}(d+p+1,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}):=\prod_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\bigg(\max_{j\in\underline{d}}\,\,\max_{0\leq n\leq d+p+1}\Big\|\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{n}}\theta_{\ell}\Big\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}_{w_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigg).

(4.7)

Then,
m
is
\Phi
-compatible with dominating weight
m^{\Phi}(x,\tau)=(1+|x|)^{p}\cdot v_{1}(\tau)

and there is a constant
C>0
, independent of
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
and
m
,
satisfying

\big\|K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\big\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}\leq C\cdot C_{\max}<\infty.

4.1
Bounding
\|K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}
via Fourier integral operators

Towards an explicit estimate for
\|K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}
, the next result
provides an estimate
in terms of families of Fourier integral operators
[
63
,
39
,
38
,
85
]

dependent on
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
.
Here and in the following, we use
e_{\tau}
,
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, as short-hand for the (
\Phi
-dependent) map

e_{\tau}\colon\quad\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C},\quad(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\mapsto e^{-2\pi i\left\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle x\rangle,\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\right\rangle}.

(4.8)

Theorem 4.5
.

Define

L_{\tau_{0}}^{(\ell)}(x,\tau):=L_{\tau_{0}}[\theta_{\ell},\theta_{3-\ell}](x,\tau):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!\!\!\frac{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau_{0})}{w(\tau_{0})}\cdot(\theta_{3-\ell}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{T}_{\tau}\theta_{\ell}})({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot e_{\tau_{0}}(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})~d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\,,

(4.9)

for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
and
x,\tau,\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
If
m
is
\Phi
-compatible with dominating weight
m^{\Phi}
, then we have

\|K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}\leq\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\left[\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\eta\in D}\big\|L_{\Phi(\eta)}^{(\ell)}\big\|_{\mathbf{L}_{M}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right],

(4.10)

with
M
as in (
4.2
).
In particular, if

\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|L_{\tau_{0}}^{(\ell)}\big\|_{\mathbf{L}_{M}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty
,
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, then
\|K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}
is finite.

We prove
Theorem

4.5
by means of two intermediate results.
First, an (elementary) lemma concerned with the
\mathcal{B}_{m}
-norm of
K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

If
m
is
\Phi
-convolution-dominated by
m^{\Phi}
, we have

\begin{split}&\|K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}\\
&\leq\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\left[\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\eta\in D}\int_{D}\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}m^{\Phi}(y\!-\!z,\Phi(\omega)\!-\!\Phi(\eta))\cdot|K_{\theta_{\ell},\theta_{3-\ell}}((y,\omega),(z,\eta))|~dy~d\omega\right]\!.\end{split}

(4.11)

Proof.

If we define
\widetilde{m}^{\Phi}(x,\tau):=\min\{m^{\Phi}(x,\tau),m^{\Phi}(-x,-\tau)\}
,
the symmetry of
m
easily shows that (
4.1
) also holds for

\widetilde{m}^{\Phi}
instead of
m^{\Phi}
.
Hence, we can assume in what follows that
m^{\Phi}
satisfies

m^{\Phi}(-x,-\tau)=m^{\Phi}(x,\tau)
for all
x,\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

For
\ell\in\{1,2\}
and
\omega,\eta\in D
, define

B_{\ell}(\omega,\eta):=\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}m^{\Phi}\bigl(y-z,\Phi(\omega)-\Phi(\eta)\bigr)\cdot\big|K_{\theta_{\ell},\theta_{3-\ell}}\big((y,\omega),(z,\eta)\big)\big|\,dy,

and let
C:=\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\eta\in D}\int_{D}B_{\ell}(\omega,\eta)\,d\omega
,
which is precisely the right-hand side of the target inequality.
Equation (
4.1
) yields

\begin{split}\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigl|(m\cdot K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}})\big((y,\omega),(z,\eta)\big)\bigr|\,dy\leq B_{1}(\omega,\eta).\end{split}

(4.12)

Next, note that
\langle\mathbf{T}_{y}f_{1},\mathbf{T}_{z}f_{2}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{T}_{-z}f_{1},\mathbf{T}_{-y}f_{2}\rangle

and
\langle f_{1},f_{2}\rangle=\overline{\langle f_{2},f_{1}\rangle}
for all
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Based on these identities and the translation-invariant structure of warped time-frequency
systems, we see

\begin{split}K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\bigl((y,\omega),(z,\eta)\bigr)&=K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\bigl((-z,\omega),(-y,\eta)\bigr)\\
&=\overline{K_{\theta_{2},\theta_{1}}\bigl((-y,\eta),(-z,\omega)\bigr)}=\overline{K_{\theta_{2},\theta_{1}}\bigl((z,\eta),(y,\omega)\bigr)}.\end{split}

(4.13)

Using these identities and renaming
\widetilde{z}=-y
and
\widetilde{y}=-z
, we see

\begin{split}&\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigl|(m\cdot K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}})\big((y,\omega),(z,\eta)\big)\bigr|\,dz\\
&\leq\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}m^{\Phi}\bigl((-z)-(-y),\Phi(\omega)-\Phi(\eta)\bigr)\cdot\bigl|K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\big((-z,\omega),(-y,\eta)\big)\bigr|\,dz\\
&=\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\widetilde{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}m^{\Phi}\bigl(\widetilde{y}-\widetilde{z},\Phi(\omega)-\Phi(\eta)\bigr)\cdot\bigl|K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\big((\widetilde{y},\omega),(\widetilde{z},\eta)\big)\bigr|\,d\widetilde{y}=B_{1}(\omega,\eta).\end{split}

(4.14)

Combining (
4.12
) and (
4.14
),
we see with notation as in (
2.13
) that

\big\|(m\cdot K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}})^{(\omega,\eta)}\big\|_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\leq B_{1}(\omega,\eta)\qquad\forall\,\omega,\eta\in D.

A simple calculation using (
4.13
)
and the symmetry
m^{\Phi}(-x,-\tau)=m^{\Phi}(x,\tau)
proves the identity

B_{1}(\omega,\eta)=B_{2}(\eta,\omega)
.
Overall, we thus see

\displaystyle\|m\cdot K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}

\displaystyle=\max\Big\{\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\eta\in D}\int_{D}\|(m\cdot K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}})^{(\omega,\eta)}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\,d\omega,\,\,\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\omega\in D}\int_{D}\|(m\cdot K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}})^{(\omega,\eta)}\|_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\,d\eta\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\eta\in D}\int_{D}B_{1}(\omega,\eta)\,d\omega,\,\,\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\eta\in D}\int_{D}B_{2}(\eta,\omega)\,d\omega\Big\},

which completes the proof.
∎

The second intermediate result expresses the integral over
D
in (
4.11
)
through the Fourier integral operators
L^{(\ell)}_{\tau_{0}}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
L_{\tau_{0}}^{(\ell)}
,
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, be as in Theorem
4.5
.
For all
(y,\omega),(z,\eta)\in\Lambda
and
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, we have

\bigl|K_{\theta_{\ell},\theta_{3-\ell}}\bigl((y,\omega),(z,\eta)\bigr)\bigr|=\sqrt{\!\frac{w(\Phi(\eta))}{w(\Phi(\omega))}}\cdot\left|L^{(\ell)}_{\Phi(\eta)}\bigl(A^{T}(\Phi(\eta))\langle z-y\rangle,\Phi(\omega)-\Phi(\eta)\bigr)\right|.

(4.15)

If
m
is
\Phi
-compatible with dominating weight
m^{\Phi}
, then we have,
for given arbitrary
\ell\in\{1,2\}
and
\eta\in D
,

\int_{D}\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}m^{\Phi}\bigl(y-z,\Phi(\omega)-\Phi(\eta)\bigr)\cdot|K_{\theta_{\ell},\theta_{3-\ell}}\bigl((y,\omega),(z,\eta)\bigr)|~dy~d\omega\leq\big\|L_{\Phi(\eta)}^{(\ell)}\big\|_{\mathbf{L}_{M}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(4.16)

with
M
as in (
4.2
).

Proof.

We provide the proof for
\ell=1
; the proof for
\ell=2
follows the same steps.
First, recall from after Equation (
3.3
) the identity

0<w(\Phi(\xi))=[\det\mathrm{D}\Phi(\xi)]^{-1}
for all
\xi\in D
.
This identity will be applied repeatedly.
To show (
4.15
), apply Plancherel’s theorem
and perform the change of variable
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}=\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\eta)
to derive

\begin{split}&\left|K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\bigl((y,\omega),(z,\eta)\bigr)\right|=\bigl|\big\langle g_{z,\eta}^{[2]},g_{y,\omega}^{[1]}\big\rangle\bigr|=\big|\big\langle\widehat{\smash{g_{z,\eta}^{[2]}}},\widehat{\smash{g_{y,\omega}^{[1]}}}\big\rangle\big|\\
&=\left|\int_{D}\frac{\theta_{2}(\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\eta))\cdot\overline{\theta_{1}(\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\omega))}}{\sqrt{w(\Phi(\eta))\cdot w(\Phi(\omega))}}\cdot e^{-2\pi i\left\langle z-y,\xi\right\rangle}~d\xi\right|\\
&=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\theta_{2}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot\overline{\theta_{1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\Phi(\eta)-\Phi(\omega))}\cdot\frac{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\Phi(\eta))}{\sqrt{w(\Phi(\eta))w(\Phi(\omega))}}\cdot e^{-2\pi i\left\langle z-y,\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\Phi(\eta))\right\rangle}~d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\right|.\end{split}

(4.17)

This easily implies (
4.15
).

To prove (
4.16
), set
\tau_{0}:=\Phi(\eta)
and note that
(
4.15
) implies that the left-hand side of
(
4.16
) satisfies

\begin{split}\circledast&:=\mathrm{LHS}\eqref{eq:kerncond_warped}\\
&=\int_{D}\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}m^{\Phi}(y-z,\Phi(\omega)-\tau_{0})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{w(\tau_{0})}{w(\Phi(\omega))}}\cdot\left|L_{\tau_{0}}^{(1)}(A^{T}(\tau_{0})\langle z-y\rangle,\Phi(\omega)-\tau_{0})\right|~dy~d\omega.\end{split}

Next, perform the change of variable
\tau=\Phi(\omega)-\tau_{0}
to obtain

\circledast=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}m^{\Phi}(y-z,\tau)\sqrt{w(\tau_{0})w(\tau+\tau_{0})}\cdot\left|L_{\tau_{0}}^{(1)}(A^{T}(\tau_{0})\langle z-y\rangle,\tau)\right|~dy~d\tau=:\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\dagger$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\dagger$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\dagger$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\dagger$\cr}}}}.

Next, perform the change of variables
x=A^{T}(\tau_{0})\langle z-y\rangle
in the inner integral
and apply the estimate
\sqrt{\frac{w(\tau+\tau_{0})}{w(\tau_{0})}}\leq\sqrt{w_{0}(\tau)}
to derive

\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\displaystyle\dagger$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\textstyle\dagger$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptstyle\dagger$\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$}}}$\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\dagger$\cr}}}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}m^{\Phi}(-A^{-T}(\tau_{0})\langle x\rangle,\tau)\cdot\sqrt{w_{0}(\tau)}\cdot\left|L_{\tau_{0}}^{(1)}(x,\tau)\right|~dx~d\tau.

Now, in the case where
\mathrm{D}\Phi
is unbounded, we are done, since in this case
Equations (
4.3
) and (
4.2
) show

m^{\Phi}(-A^{-T}(\tau_{0})\langle x\rangle,\tau)\cdot\sqrt{w_{0}(\tau)}=m^{\Phi}(0,\tau)\cdot\sqrt{w_{0}(\tau)}\leq M(x,\tau).

For the case that
\mathrm{D}\Phi
is bounded,
recall from (
3.3
)
that
A^{-T}(\tau_{0})=A^{-T}(\Phi(\eta))=[\mathrm{D}\Phi]^{T}(\eta)
and thus

|A^{-T}(\tau_{0})\langle x\rangle|=|\mathrm{D}\Phi^{T}(\eta)\langle x\rangle|\leq R|x|

by choice of
R
in (
4.2
).
Therefore, by choice of
M
, we see

m^{\Phi}(-A^{-T}(\tau_{0})\langle x\rangle,\tau)\cdot\sqrt{w_{0}(\tau)}\leq M(x,\tau).\qed

We now obtain Eq. (
4.10
) in Theorem
4.5

simply by inserting Eq. (
4.16
) into Eq. (
4.11
).

4.2
Uniform integrability of the integral kernels
L^{(\ell)}_{\tau_{0}}

To control
\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\eta\in D}\|L^{(\ell)}_{\Phi(\eta)}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{1}_{M}}
,
we find that
k
-admissibility of the warping function
\Phi
is crucial.
The remainder of this subsection is dedicated to proving
Theorem
4.8
below,
which will in turn be central to proving Theorem
4.4
.

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
\Phi
be a
k
-admissible warping function with control weight
v_{0}
.
Furthermore, let
w_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be continuous
and submultiplicative and such that
w_{1}(-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=w_{1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})
for all
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Define

w_{2}:\quad\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+},\quad{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\mapsto w_{1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot[v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{d+3k},

assume that
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are such that

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{n}}\theta_{\ell}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{w_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad\text{ for all }j\in\underline{d},\ \ell\in\{1,2\},\ 0\leq n\leq k,

(4.18)

and recall from Equation (
4.7
), that

C_{\max}=\prod_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\Bigg(\max_{j\in\underline{d}}\,\,\max_{0\leq n\leq k}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{n}}\theta_{\ell}\bigg\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}_{w_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Bigg).

Then, with
e_{\tau}
as defined in Equation (
4.8
)
and
L_{\tau_{0}}^{(\ell)}
as in
Theorem

4.5

there exists a constant

C=C(d,k,v_{0})>0
satisfying
for all
x,\tau,\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\ell\in\{1,2\}
the estimate

\left|L_{\tau_{0}}^{(\ell)}(x,\tau)\right|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau_{0})}{w(\tau_{0})}\left(\theta_{3-\ell}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{T}_{\tau}\theta_{\ell}}\right)\!({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\,e_{\tau_{0}}(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})~d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\right|\leq C\cdot C_{\max}\cdot(1+|x|)^{-k}\cdot[w_{1}(\tau)]^{-1}.

(4.19)

Remark
.

In Section
6
, we will apply
Theorem

4.8
in a setting
in which
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
depend on
x,\tau,\tau_{0}
.
We suggest that the reader keeps this potential dependency in mind.

In a first step, we derive a number of important consequences of Definition
4.2

that will be used repeatedly.

Lemma 4.9
.

If
\Phi
is a
0
-admissible warping function with control weight
v_{0}
,
then
\Phi
is a warping function in the sense of Definition
3.1
.
In particular,
w=\det A
is
w_{0}
-moderate with
w_{0}=v_{0}^{d}
, i.e.

\displaystyle w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\leq w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot[v_{0}(\tau)]^{d}\qquad\forall\,\tau,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

(4.20)

and

\displaystyle\|A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\|\leq\|A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\|\cdot v_{0}(\tau)\qquad\forall\,\tau,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(4.21)

Additionally, for arbitrary
\gamma\in S^{d-1}
and
\tau,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

[v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-\tau)]^{-1}\leq\|A^{-1}(\tau)A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\|^{-1}\leq|\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(\tau-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle\gamma\rangle|\leq\|A^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot A(\tau)\|\leq v_{0}(\tau-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})

(4.22)

and

\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=\phi_{\tau_{0}+\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-\tau)\cdot\phi_{\tau_{0}}(\tau).

(4.23)

Finally, we have

[v_{0}(\tau)]^{-1}\cdot|\gamma|\leq|\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(\tau)\langle\gamma\rangle|\leq v_{0}(\tau)\cdot|\gamma|\qquad\forall\,\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ and }\tau,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(4.24)

Proof.

To show that
\Phi
is a warping function, we need only verify moderateness of
w=\det A
.
To prove this moderateness, apply
Hadamard’s inequality

|\det M|\leq\|M\|^{d}=\|M^{T}\|^{d}
(see
[
79
, Chapter 75]
)
for
M\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, combined with (
4.5
)
(for
\alpha=0
) to see that

\frac{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)}{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})}=\det\left([A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{-1}A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\right)\leq\big\|[A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{-1}A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\big\|^{d}=\big\|[\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(\tau)]^{T}\big\|^{d}\leq[v_{0}(\tau)]^{d}.

Hence, we obtain (
4.20
).
Moreover,

\|A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\|=\|A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})A^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\|\leq\|A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\|\cdot\|[\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(\tau)]^{T}\|\smash{\overset{\eqref{eq:PhiHigherDerivativeEstimate}}{\leq}}\|A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\|\cdot v_{0}(\tau),

proving (
4.21
).
To show (
4.22
), first note for
\gamma\in S^{d-1}

and any
M\in\mathrm{GL}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
that
|M\gamma|\geq\|M^{-1}\|^{-1}
,
and then apply (
4.5
) twice:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|[\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-\tau)]^{T}\big\|^{-1}=\|A^{-1}(\tau)A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\|^{-1}=\big\|[A^{T}(\tau)\cdot A^{-T}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{-1}\big\|^{-1}=\big\|[\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(\tau-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{-1}\big\|^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq|\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(\tau-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\gamma|\leq\|\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(\tau-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\|=\|A^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot A(\tau)\|

\displaystyle\leq v_{0}(\tau-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}),\quad\text{ for all }\quad{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}},\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad\gamma\in S^{d-1}.

Finally, assertion (
4.23
) is easily verified using direct computation,
and
[v_{0}(\tau)]^{-1}\leq|\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(\tau)\cdot\gamma|\leq v_{0}(\tau)

for
|\gamma|=1
is obtained from (
4.22
) through the bijective map

\tau\mapsto\tau-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}
and using that
v_{0}
is radial.
This proves (
4.24
).
∎

Lemma
4.9
shows that
w
is
v_{0}^{d}
-moderate.
The next result provides
v_{0}^{d}
-moderateness (up to a constant) for the partial derivatives of
w
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\Phi
be a
k
-admissible warping function with control weight
v_{0}
.
For every
j\in\underline{d}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
n\leq k
, we have

\left|\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\right|\leq D_{n}\cdot[v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{d}\cdot w(\tau),\text{ for all }{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}},\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(4.25)

with
D_{n}:=D_{n}(d):=d!\cdot d^{n}
.

Proof.

We begin by rewriting
\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)

using some simple properties of determinants:

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)&=\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}\det(A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau))=\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}\det(A^{T}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau))\\
&=\det(A^{T}(\tau))\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}\det(A^{T}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)A^{-T}(\tau))\overset{\eqref{eq:PhiDefinition}}{=}w(\tau)\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}\det(\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})).\end{split}

Let
S_{d}
be the set of permutations on
\underline{d}
.
Then, the definition of the determinant yields

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}\det(\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}))=\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}\left[\sum_{\sigma\in S_{d}}\mathop{\operatorname{sgn}}(\sigma)\prod_{i=1}^{d}[\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]_{i,\sigma(i)}\right]=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{d}}\mathop{\operatorname{sgn}}(\sigma)\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}\prod_{i=1}^{d}[\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]_{i,\sigma(i)}.

The general Leibniz rule for products with
d
terms shows

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}\prod_{i=1}^{d}[\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]_{i,\sigma(i)}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},\dots,m_{d}\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\\
m_{1}+\ldots+m_{d}=n\end{subarray}}\binom{n}{m_{1},\ldots,m_{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\partial^{m_{i}}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m_{i}}}[\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]_{i,\sigma(i)},

where
\binom{n}{m_{1},\ldots,m_{d}}:=\frac{n!}{m_{1}!\cdots m_{d}!}

is the usual multinomial coefficient.
Moreover, the estimate (
4.5
) yields

\left|\frac{\partial^{m_{i}}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m_{i}}}[\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]_{i,\sigma(i)}\right|\leq\left\|\partial^{m_{i}e_{j}}\phi_{\tau}\left({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\right)\right\|\leq v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}).

Altogether, we obtain

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\right|&=\left|w(\tau)\cdot\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}^{n}_{j}}\det(\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}))\right|\\
&\leq w(\tau)\cdot[v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{d}\cdot\sum_{\sigma\in S_{d}}\,\,\,\sum_{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{d}=n}\binom{n}{m_{1},\ldots,m_{d}}\\
&=d!\cdot d^{n}\cdot[v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{d}\cdot w(\tau)=D_{n}\cdot[v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{d}\cdot w(\tau),\end{split}

where we used
|S_{d}|=d!
and the multinomial theorem
(see e.g.
[
45
, Exercise 2(a)]
), i.e.

\sum_{m_{1}+\ldots+m_{d}=n}\binom{n}{m_{1},\ldots,m_{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{d}a_{i}^{m_{i}}=(a_{1}+\ldots+a_{d})^{n},\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{N},\ (a_{i})_{i\in\underline{d}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

for
a_{1},\ldots,a_{d}=1
.
Thus, the proof is complete.
∎

We now turn our attention towards the Fourier integral operators
L^{(\ell)}_{\tau_{0}}
defined in (
4.9
).
We will obtain the desired integrability with respect to
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

by means of an integration by parts argument of the kind well-known
for establishing the smoothness-decay duality of a function and its Fourier transform,
as well as the asymptotic behavior of oscillatory integrals, cf.
[
85
, Chapter VIII]
.
An additional complication in our setting is that we require a uniform estimate
over all
L^{(\ell)}_{\tau_{0}}
,
\ell\in\{1,2\}
,
\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

For now, we replace

\frac{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau_{0})}{w(\tau_{0})}\left(\theta_{2}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{T}_{\tau}\theta_{1}}\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})

in (
4.9
) by an unspecific, compactly supported function
g\in\mathcal{C}^{k}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, i.e., we consider

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot e_{\tau_{0}}(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})~d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}},\text{ recalling }e_{\tau}(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=e^{-2\pi i\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle x\rangle,\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\rangle},\quad\text{ for all }x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}},\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(4.26)

Note that, with
f=e_{\tau}(x,\bullet)
, we have

\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}}f({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=-2\pi i\cdot\left\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle x\rangle,\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}}\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\right\rangle\cdot e_{\tau}(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=-2\pi i\cdot(\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot x)_{j}\cdot e_{\tau}(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}).

The final equality can be verified by observing

\begin{split}\big\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle\eta\rangle,\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}}\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\big\rangle&=\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle\eta\rangle,\mathrm{D}\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\langle e_{j}\rangle\rangle\\
&=\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle\eta\rangle,A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\langle e_{j}\rangle\rangle=(\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot\eta)_{j},\end{split}

(4.27)

which motivates the definition of
\phi_{\tau}
.
Provided
(\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot x)_{j}\neq 0
on the support of
g
, we obtain, with

\tilde{g}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=\left(-2\pi i\cdot(\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle x\rangle)_{j}\right)^{-1}\cdot g({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}),

\int g({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})f({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}=\int\tilde{g}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}}f({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}=-\int\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}}\tilde{g}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})f({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}},

where the last equality is obtained through integration by parts.

For fixed
x,\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
j\in\underline{d}
and all
g\in\mathcal{C}^{k}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

(\phi_{\tau}(\cdot)\langle x\rangle)_{j}\neq 0
on the support of
g
,
we define the differential operator
\Square_{j,\tau,x}
by

\left(\Square_{j,\tau,x}\,g\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}):=(2\pi i)^{-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}}\left[\frac{g({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})}{(\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle x\rangle)_{j}}\right]=(2\pi i|x|)^{-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}}\left[\frac{g({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})}{(\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle\rho_{x}\rangle)_{j}}\right],

(4.28)

where
\rho_{x}\in S^{d-1}
with
x=|x|\rho_{x}
.
We can rewrite the integral in (
4.26
) as

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\,e_{\tau}(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!\left(\Square_{j,\tau,x}\,g\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})e_{\tau}(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\!\left(\Square_{j,\tau,x}^{n}\,g\right)\!\!({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\,e_{\tau}(x,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})d{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}},\text{ for }n\leq k.

(4.29)

where
\Square_{j,\tau,x}^{n}
denotes
n
-fold application of
\Square_{j,\tau,x}
.

By (
4.28
), each application of
\Square_{j,\tau,x}
provides additional,
linear decay with respect to
|x|
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
For a given pair
(\Phi,\theta)
of warping function and prototype, however,
we cannot expect the support restriction required for the application
of the differential operator
\Square_{j,\tau,x}
, i.e.,

(\phi_{\tau}(\cdot)\langle x\rangle)_{j}\neq 0
on the support of
g
, to hold.
To account for this, we decompose

g_{\tau,\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}):=\frac{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau_{0})}{w(\tau_{0})}\left(\theta_{2}\cdot\overline{\mathbf{T}_{\tau}\theta_{1}}\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})

into compactly supported functions, such that each of them allows
the application of
\Square_{j,\tau,x}
, for some
j\in\underline{d}
.
Therefore, our next steps are:

•

Step 1:
Find a suitable splitting

g_{\tau,\tau_{0}}=\sum_{i\in I}g_{i,\tau,\tau_{0}}=\sum_{i\in I}\varphi_{i}g_{\tau,\tau_{0}}

(with
(\varphi_{i})_{i}
only depending on
\tau_{0}
) into compactly supported elements

g_{i,\tau,\tau_{0}}=\varphi_{i}g_{\tau,\tau_{0}}
,
such that, for any fixed
\rho_{x}\in S^{d-1}
,
\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
i\in I
,
there is an index
j=j(\rho_{x},\tau_{0},i)\in\underline{d}

and a positive function
\tilde{v}
(
independent
of
i,\rho_{x},\tau_{0},\tau
),
such that

|(\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle\rho_{x}\rangle)_{j}|\geq\tilde{v}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})>0

for
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}\varphi_{i}\supset\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(g_{i,\tau,\tau_{0}})
.
Besides being able to apply
\Square_{j,\tau_{0},x}
, this property lets us control
the growth of
\frac{1}{(\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle\rho_{x}\rangle)_{j}}

independently of the orientation
\rho_{x}\in S^{d-1}
and of
\tau,\tau_{0}
.

•

Step 2:
Estimate
\left(\Square_{j,\tau_{0},x}^{n}\,\,g_{i,\tau,\tau_{0}}\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})
,
for
x=|x|\cdot\rho_{x}\neq 0
, independently of
i,\rho_{x},\tau_{0}
.
In fact, this estimate will exhibit rapid decay with respect to
|x|

and depend boundedly on the derivative of
g_{i,\tau,\tau_{0}}
,
which can be used to obtain decay with respect to
|\tau|
.

Towards Step 1, we introduce a specific family of coverings
in the following lemma.
The smooth splitting of
g_{\tau,\tau_{0}}
into the building blocks

g_{i,\tau,\tau_{0}}=\varphi_{i}g_{\tau,\tau_{0}}
, see Lemma
4.13
, is provided
by a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}
partition of unity
(\varphi_{i})_{i}
with respect to these coverings,
introduced in Lemma
4.12
.
Lemmas
4.14
and
4.15

take care of Step 2.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\Phi
be a
1
-admissible warping function with control weight
v_{0}
.
For any
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0},\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the following are true:

1.

The family
\left(U_{j}^{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0},\tau_{0})}\right)_{j\in\underline{d}}
defined by

U_{j}^{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0},\tau_{0})}:=\left\{\gamma\in S^{d-1}~:~\left|\left(\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\langle\gamma\rangle\right)_{j}\right|>\frac{1}{2d}\left|\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\langle\gamma\rangle\right|\right\}

is a covering of
S^{d-1}
.

2.

For any
\delta>0
satisfying
\delta\cdot v_{0}(\delta/(4d)\cdot e_{1})\leq 1/\sqrt{d}

and arbitrary
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in B_{\delta/(4d)}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})

and
\gamma\in U_{j}^{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0},\tau_{0})}
, we have

\big|\left(\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle\gamma\rangle\right)_{j}\big|\geq C_{\delta}\cdot[v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{-1},

with
C_{\delta}:=C_{\delta}(d,v_{0}):=\bigl[4d\cdot v_{0}(\delta/(4d)\cdot e_{1})\bigr]^{-1}
.

Remark
.

If
\delta\leq\min\bigl\{1,1/(\sqrt{d}\cdot v_{0}(e_{1}/(4d)))\bigr\}
, then

\delta\cdot v_{0}(\delta/(4d)\cdot e_{1})\leq\delta\cdot v_{0}(e_{1}/(4d))\leq 1/\sqrt{d}
.
Hence, the condition of Part (2) of the lemma is satisfied for all sufficiently
small
\delta>0
.

Proof.

Part (1) does not use any of the properties of
\Phi
,
except that
\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\in\mathrm{GL}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
:
We simply note that any
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
satisfies

\left|z\right|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left|z_{j}\right|\leq d\cdot\max\bigl\{\left|z_{j}\right|\colon j\in\underline{d}\bigr\}<2d\cdot\max\bigl\{\left|z_{j}\right|\colon j\in\underline{d}\bigr\}.

Hence, there is some
j\in\underline{d}
with
|z_{j}|>\frac{1}{2d}\cdot|z|
.
Now apply this to
z=\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\langle\gamma\rangle
, noting
that
z\neq 0
since
\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\in\mathrm{GL}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and
\gamma\in S^{d-1}
.

For part (2), let
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in B_{\delta/(4d)}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})

and
\gamma\in U_{j}^{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0},\tau_{0})}\subset S^{d-1}
be arbitrary.
The triangle inequality provides

\displaystyle|(\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle\gamma\rangle)_{j}|

\displaystyle\geq|(\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\langle\gamma\rangle)_{j}|-|(\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle\gamma\rangle-\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\langle\gamma\rangle)_{j}|

\displaystyle({\scriptstyle{\text{since }\gamma\in U_{j}^{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0},\tau_{0})}}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{|\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\langle\gamma\rangle|}{2d}-|\left(\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})-\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\right)\langle\gamma\rangle|.

Note that

\begin{split}\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})-\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})&=\left(\phi_{\tau_{0}+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})-\mathrm{id}\right)\phi_{\tau_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}),\end{split}

(4.30)

where we used the identity (
4.23
) of Lemma
4.9
,
with
\tau={\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}
.

To estimate the first factor on the right-hand side of (
4.30
),
recall that
\phi_{\tau_{0}+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}}(0)=\mathrm{id}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathrm{id}-\phi_{\tau_{0}+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\big\|

\displaystyle=\big\|\phi_{\tau_{0}+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}}(0)-\phi_{\tau_{0}+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})\big\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=s}\big[\phi_{\tau_{0}+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}}(t({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}))\big]\,ds\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\big\|(\partial_{\ell}\,\,\phi_{\tau_{0}+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}})(s({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}))\big\|\cdot\bigl|({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0})_{\ell}\bigr|\,ds=:(\ast).

We now rewrite this expression further, recalling that
v_{0}
is radially increasing and applying
the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and inequality (
4.5
):

\displaystyle(\ast)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{CS}}{\leq}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}|\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left|\left(\begin{matrix}\left\|(\partial_{1}\,\phi_{\tau_{0}+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}})(t({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}))\right\|\\
\vdots\\
\left\|(\partial_{d}\,\phi_{\tau_{0}+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}})(t({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{0}))\right\|\end{matrix}\right)\right|
