(\ell,k)\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be such that
(y,\omega)\in V_{\ell,k}^{\delta}
.
Then
\delta k/\sqrt{d}\in\Phi(\omega)+\delta\cdot B_{1}(0)

and by extension of that argument,

Q_{k}^{\delta}\subset\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(\omega)+2\delta B_{1}(0))=\mathbf{Q}_{\omega}^{\delta}
,
which proves the first part of the claim.

Next, for
(x,\xi)\in V_{\ell,k}^{\delta}
, we have

|A^{T}(\delta k/\sqrt{d})\left\langle x-y\right\rangle|<2\delta
, since

x,y\in A^{-T}(\delta k/\sqrt{d})\left\langle\delta B_{1}(\ell/\sqrt{d})\right\rangle
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\left|A^{T}(\Phi(\omega))\left\langle x-y\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|A^{T}(\Phi(\omega))A^{-T}(\delta k/\sqrt{d})A^{T}(\delta k/\sqrt{d})\left\langle x-y\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle<2\delta\cdot\big\|A^{T}(\Phi(\omega))A^{-T}(\delta k/\sqrt{d})\big\|=2\delta\cdot\big\|\phi_{\delta k/\sqrt{d}}\bigl(\Phi(\omega)-\delta k/\sqrt{d}\bigr)\big\|

\displaystyle({\scriptstyle\text{cf.~Eq.~\eqref{eq:PhiHigherDerivativeEstimate}}})

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta\cdot v_{0}(\Phi(\omega)-\delta k/\sqrt{d})\leq 2\delta\cdot v_{0}(\delta),

which shows

x-y\in A^{-T}(\Phi(\omega))\left\langle 2\delta v_{0}(\delta)B_{1}(0)\right\rangle
,
and thus
x\in y+\mathbf{P}_{\omega}^{\delta}
, as desired.
∎

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
\Phi
be a
0
-admissible warping function with control weight
v_{0}
.
Let further
m_{0}:\Lambda\times\Lambda\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be continuous and symmetric,
with
1\leq m_{0}(\lambda,\rho)\leq C^{(0)}\cdot m_{0}(\lambda,{\nu})\cdot m_{0}({\nu},\rho)
,
for all
\lambda,\rho,{\nu}\in\Lambda
and some
C^{(0)}\geq 1
, satisfy

m_{0}((y,\xi),(z,\eta))\leq(1+|y-z|)^{p}\cdot\zeta_{1}\bigl(\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\eta)\bigr)\quad\forall\,(y,\xi),(z,\eta)\in\Lambda.

Here,
p=0
if
R_{\Phi}=\sup_{\xi\in D}\|\mathrm{D}\Phi(\xi)\|=\infty

and
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
otherwise, and
\zeta_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is a continuous function
with
\zeta_{1}(-\tau)=\zeta_{1}(\tau)
for all
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Define, for some arbitrary, fixed
{\nu}\in\Lambda
,

\begin{split}u:\quad\Lambda\to\mathbb{R}^{+},&\quad\lambda\mapsto m_{0}(\lambda,{\nu})\qquad\text{and}\\
v:\quad\Lambda\to\mathbb{R}^{+},&\quad(y,\xi)\mapsto u(y,\xi)\cdot\max\big\{w(\Phi(\xi)),[w(\Phi(\xi))]^{-1}\big\}\end{split}

and let
m_{v}
be as in Equation (
2.10
).
Then
u
is
\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}^{\delta}
-moderate, for any
\delta>0
, and

m_{0}
and
m_{v}
are
\Phi
-convolution-dominated by
(1+|\bullet|)^{p}\cdot\zeta_{1}(\bullet)

and
m_{v}^{\Phi}:=(1+|\bullet|)^{p}\cdot\zeta_{2}(\bullet)
, where
\zeta_{2}=v_{0}^{d}\cdot\zeta_{1}
.
In particular, items (1)-(3) of Assumption
2.11
are satisfied.

Proof.

Proposition

5.2
provides product-admissibility of
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi}
,
such that item (1) of Assumption
2.11
is satisfied.
Item (3) is a direct consequence of the symmetry of
m_{0}
:

m_{0}(\lambda,\rho)\leq C^{(0)}\cdot m_{0}(\lambda,{\nu})\cdot m_{0}({\nu},\rho)=C^{(0)}\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot u(\rho).

To show
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi}
-moderateness of
u

(which coincides with item (2) of Assumption
2.11
), observe that

\frac{u(\lambda)}{u(\rho)}\leq C^{(0)}\frac{m_{0}(\lambda,\rho)m_{0}(\rho,{\nu})}{m_{0}(\rho,{\nu})}=C^{(0)}m_{0}(\lambda,\rho).

(5.8)

If
\lambda=(y,\xi)
and
\rho=(z,\eta)
are both contained in
V^{\delta}_{\ell,k}
,
for some
\ell,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, then
|\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\eta)|<\delta
, and

|y-z|\leq\delta\cdot\|A^{-T}(\delta k/\sqrt{d})\|\leq\delta\cdot R_{\Phi},\quad\text{if }R_{\Phi}<\infty.

Hence, and

\frac{u(y,\xi)}{u(z,\eta)}\leq C^{(0)}m_{0}((y,\xi),(z,\eta))\leq C^{(0)}(1+\delta R_{\Phi})^{p}\cdot\zeta_{1}(\delta),

independent of
\ell,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
If
R_{\Phi}=\infty
, then

\frac{u(y,\xi)}{u(z,\eta)}\leq C^{(0)}m_{0}((y,\xi),(z,\eta))\leq C^{(0)}\zeta_{1}(\delta)

instead.

That
m_{0}
is
\Phi
-convolution-dominated by
(1+|\bullet|)^{p}\cdot\zeta_{1}(\bullet)
is immediate.
To prove that
m_{v}
is
\Phi
-convolution-dominated by
m_{v}^{\Phi}
, observe

\frac{\max\big\{w(\tau),[w(\tau)]^{-1}\big\}}{\max\big\{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}),[w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{-1}\big\}}\leq\max\left\{\frac{w(\tau)}{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})},\frac{w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})}{w(\tau)}\right\}\leq v_{0}^{d}(\tau-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}),\text{ for all }\tau,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Combine the above with (
5.8
), such that

\frac{v(y,\Phi(\xi))}{v(z,\Phi(\eta))}\leq C^{(0)}\cdot m_{0}((y,\Phi(\xi)),(z,\Phi(\eta)))\cdot v_{0}^{d}(\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\eta)).\qed

6
Controlling the
\mathcal{B}_{m}
-norm of the oscillation

In this section, we employ the
\Phi
-induced
\delta
-fine phase-space coverings
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi}
,
constructed in the previous section, to derive conditions concerning the prototype function

\theta
which ensure that
\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}<\infty

with
\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}\rightarrow 0
as
\delta\rightarrow 0
. We will obtain the following result.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
\Phi
be a
(d+p+1)
-admissible warping function with control weight
v_{0}
,
where
p=0
if
R_{\Phi}=\sup_{\xi\in D}\|\mathrm{D}\Phi(\xi)\|=\infty
and
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
otherwise.
Let furthermore
m:\Lambda\times\Lambda\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a symmetric weight that satisfies

m((y,\xi),(z,\eta))\leq(1+|y-z|)^{p}\cdot v_{1}(\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\eta)),\forall\,y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ and }\xi,\eta\in D,

(6.1)

for some continuous and submultiplicative weight
v_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}

satisfying
v_{1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=v_{1}(-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})
for all
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Finally, with

w_{2}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\mapsto(1+|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}|)^{d+1}\cdot v_{1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot[v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})]^{9d/2+3p+3},

assume that
\theta\in\mathcal{C}^{d+p+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{(d+p+1)-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{(d+p+1)-n}}\theta\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{w_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad\text{ for all }i\in\underline{d},\,\,\,0\leq n\leq d+p+1.

Then, with

\Gamma:\Lambda\times\Lambda\to\mathbb{C},\bigl((y,\omega),(z,\eta)\bigr)\mapsto e^{-2\pi i\langle y-z,\omega\rangle},

and
\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}^{\delta}=(V_{\ell,k}^{\delta})_{\ell,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}

the
\Phi
-induced
\delta
-fine covering:

\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}<\infty\quad\text{for all }\delta>0\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}\overset{\delta\rightarrow 0}{\rightarrow}0.

(6.2)

Remark 6.2
.

The conditions of
Theorem

6.1
are largely the same
as those for
Theorem

4.4
.
The only difference is the appearance of an additional factor
v_{0}^{n}
, for certain
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
in the conditions on
\theta
.
Since
v_{0}\geq v_{0}(0)
, the conditions of
Theorem

6.1

imply those of
Theorem

4.4
.

To prove
Theorem

6.1
, we study the second component of the oscillation, i.e.,

g_{\lambda}-\Gamma(\lambda,\rho)g_{\rho},

for
\rho\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\lambda}
.
If we can bound certain weighted
\mathbf{L}^{2}
-norms of this difference and its derivatives
uniformly in
\lambda\in\Lambda
and
\rho\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\lambda}
,
then we can show that
{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\in\mathcal{B}_{m}
by a slight variation on
Theorem

4.4
.
In fact, the estimates we obtain converge to
0
for
\delta\to 0
,
such that we naturally obtain the second part of
Equation

6.2
as well.

6.1
Local behavior of the oscillating component

In order to rely on the machinery we already developed in Section
4
,
it will be useful to rewrite

g_{\lambda}-\Gamma(\lambda,\rho)g_{\rho}

as the warping of a function
\tilde{\theta}_{\lambda,\rho}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}

(dependent on
\lambda,\rho\in\Lambda
) derived from the prototype
\theta
.

Proposition 6.3
.

For
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
open, let
\Lambda=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times D
, and define
the phase function
\Gamma
via

\Gamma:\Lambda\times\Lambda\to\mathbb{C},((y,\omega),(z,\eta))\mapsto e^{-2\pi i\langle y-z,\omega\rangle}.

(6.3)

Let
\Phi:D\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a warping function, assume
\theta\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

and denote
(g_{y,\omega})_{(y,\omega)\in\Lambda}=\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi)
as usual.
Then the identity

\widehat{g_{y,\omega}}-\Gamma((y,\omega),(z,\eta))\widehat{g_{z,\eta}}=e^{-2\pi i\langle y,\cdot\rangle}\cdot\left(w(\Phi(\omega))^{-1/2}\cdot\left(\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\omega)}\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}\right)\circ\Phi\right),

(6.4)

holds for all
(y,\omega),(z,\eta)\in\Lambda
, with

\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}:=\left(\theta-\sqrt{\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}}\cdot\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\Phi(\omega),A^{T}(\Phi(\omega))\langle y-z\rangle}\left(\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\eta)-\Phi(\omega)}\theta\right)\right)\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}.

(6.5)

The operator
\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
in Equation
6.5
is a multiplication operator defined by

\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}f:=e^{2\pi i\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle\varepsilon\rangle,\Phi^{-1}(\cdot+\tau)-\Phi^{-1}(\tau)\rangle}\cdot f\quad\text{ for all }\quad f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}\text{ and }\tau,\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(6.6)

Proof.

To see that
\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}
, note that

\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\eta)-\Phi(\omega)}
and
\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
are bounded operators on
\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}
and that

\sqrt{w(\Phi(\omega))/w(\Phi(\eta))}
is finite for all
\omega,\eta\in D
.
Here, boundedness of
\mathbf{T}_{x}
on
\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}
is a consequence of
(
3.5
), since
w_{0}
is submultiplicative.
To prove (
6.4
), note that, by definition,

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{g_{y,\omega}}-\Gamma((y,\omega),(z,\eta))\widehat{g_{z,\eta}}\right)(\xi)&=e^{-2\pi i\langle y,\xi\rangle}g_{\omega}(\xi)-e^{-2\pi i\langle y-z,\omega\rangle}e^{-2\pi i\langle z,\xi\rangle}g_{\eta}(\xi)\\
&=e^{-2\pi i\langle y,\xi\rangle}\left(g_{\omega}-e^{-2\pi i\langle y-z,\omega-\cdot\rangle}g_{\eta}\right)(\xi),\end{split}

and furthermore

\displaystyle g_{\omega}-e^{-2\pi i\langle y-z,\omega-\cdot\rangle}g_{\eta}

\displaystyle=w(\Phi(\omega))^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\omega)}\theta\right)\circ\Phi-e^{-2\pi i\langle y-z,\omega-\cdot\rangle}w(\Phi(\eta))^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\eta)}\theta\right)\circ\Phi

\displaystyle=w(\Phi(\omega))^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\omega)}\theta-\sqrt{\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}}\cdot e^{2\pi i\langle y-z,\Phi^{-1}(\cdot)-\omega\rangle}\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\eta)}\theta\right)\circ\Phi

\displaystyle=w(\Phi(\omega))^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\omega)}\left(\theta-\sqrt{\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}}\cdot e^{2\pi i\langle y-z,\Phi^{-1}(\cdot+\Phi(\omega))-\omega\rangle}\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\eta)-\Phi(\omega)}\theta\right)\right)\circ\Phi

\displaystyle=w(\Phi(\omega))^{-1/2}\left(\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\omega)}\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}\right)\circ\Phi.\qed

Now that we can express

g_{\lambda}-\Gamma(\lambda,\rho)g_{\rho}

through
\tilde{\theta}(\lambda,\rho)
, we aim to derive conditions on
\theta
, such that
Lemma
4.8
can be applied
with
\theta_{1}=\theta,\theta_{2}=\tilde{\theta}(\lambda,\rho)
.
In particular, we investigate the (uniform) continuity of the map
(\tau,\varepsilon)\mapsto\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
,
in the next lemma.
Here,
\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
is considered as an operator on
\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for suitable weights
\tilde{w}
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
q\in[1,\infty)
and let
\tilde{w}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a continuous weight function.
Furthermore, assume that
\Phi
is a
k
-admissible warping function with control weight
v_{0}
.

The operator
\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}~:~\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\tau,\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, given by (
6.6
), is well-defined and has the following properties:

(1)

If
\vartheta\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

with
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\vartheta)\subset\overline{B_{\delta}}(0)
for some
\delta>0
, then

\|\vartheta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\leq\sqrt{2[1-\cos\left(\pi\cdot\min\{1,2|\varepsilon|\delta v_{0}(\delta)\}\right)]}\cdot\|\vartheta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}.

(6.7)

(2)

If
\vartheta\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then

\sup\limits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\vartheta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\overset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\rightarrow}0.

(3)

If
\vartheta\in\mathcal{C}^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some
0\leq m\leq k+1
,
and if
j\in\underline{d}
with

v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{ for all }0\leq n\leq m,

(6.8)

then

\frac{\partial^{\ell}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\ell}}\vartheta\in\mathbf{L}_{\tilde{w}}^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for
0\leq\ell\leq m
,

\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for all
\tau,\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and

\sup\limits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\vartheta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\overset{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\rightarrow}0.

Furthermore, for each
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, there is a constant
C_{m,\varepsilon_{0}}>0

satisfying for all
|\varepsilon|\leq\varepsilon_{0}
that

\sup_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\leq\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\vartheta\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}+C_{m,\varepsilon_{0}}\cdot|\varepsilon|\cdot\sum_{m=1}^{m}\left\|v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}<\infty.

(6.9)

Proof.

Assumption (
6.8
) implies

\frac{\partial^{\ell}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\ell}}\vartheta\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for all
0\leq\ell\leq n
, since
v_{0}
is radially increasing.
Now, to prove (1), note for arbitrary
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
that

|\vartheta({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})-(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})|=\left|1-e^{2\pi i\langle\varepsilon,A^{-1}(\tau)(\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)-\Phi^{-1}(\tau))\rangle}\right|\cdot|\vartheta({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})|,

where
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}\vartheta\subset\overline{B_{\delta}}(0)
, such that it suffices to estimate this expression for

|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}|\leq\delta
.
We begin by expressing the difference
\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)-\Phi^{-1}(\tau)

through the Jacobian
A=\mathrm{D}\Phi^{-1}
of
\Phi^{-1}
by using the directional derivative.
This furnishes the following estimate:

\begin{split}|A^{-1}(\tau)(\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)-\Phi^{-1}(\tau))|&=\left|\int_{0}^{1}A^{-1}(\tau)A(\tau+r{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\rangle~dr\right|\\
&\leq|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}|\cdot\max\limits_{r\in[0,1]}\|A^{-1}(\tau)A(\tau+r{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\|\overset{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\overline{B_{\delta}}(0)}{\leq}\delta\cdot v_{0}(\delta),\end{split}

where we used (
4.5
) in the last step.
Therefore,

|\langle\varepsilon,A^{-1}(\tau)(\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)-\Phi^{-1}(\tau))\rangle|\leq|\varepsilon|\cdot\delta\cdot v_{0}(\delta).

Next, a simple calculation shows that
|1-e^{\pi ir}|=\sqrt{2[1-\cos(\pi r)]}
,
which is an even function that is increasing on
[0,1]
and converges to
0
for
r\rightarrow 0
.
Thus, we obtain

|(\vartheta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})|\leq\sqrt{2\left[1-\cos(\pi\cdot 2|\varepsilon|\delta v_{0}(\delta))\right]}\cdot|\vartheta({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})|

for all
0\leq|\varepsilon|\leq\frac{1}{2\delta v_{0}(\delta)}
.
For
|\varepsilon|>(2\delta v_{0}(\delta))^{-1}
apply the trivial estimate

|1-e^{\pi ir}|\leq 2=\sqrt{2[1-\cos(\pi)]}
instead.
This easily yields (
6.7
), in fact for
any solid Banach space
X
, and not only for
\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}
.

To prove (2), note that for a given
\vartheta\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
we have
\|\vartheta-\vartheta_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty

for the sequence
\vartheta_{n}=\vartheta\cdot{\mathds{1}}_{\overline{B_{n}}(0)}
,
by the dominated convergence theorem.
Furthermore, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\begin{split}\sup\limits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\vartheta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}&\leq\|\vartheta-\vartheta_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}+\sup\limits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\|\vartheta_{n}-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}+\|\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n}-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\right)\\
&=2\|\vartheta-\vartheta_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}+\sup\limits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\vartheta_{n}-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}.\end{split}

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, we can choose
\varepsilon_{n}>0
such that

3\|\vartheta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\cdot\sqrt{2[1-\cos\left(\pi\cdot\min\{1,2|\varepsilon_{n}|nv_{0}(n)\right)\}}<\varepsilon_{0}.

Noting that
\mathop{\operatorname{supp}}(\vartheta_{n})\subset\overline{B_{n}}(0)
by definition,
we can now apply (
6.7
) with
\delta=n

and any
\varepsilon\in\overline{B_{\varepsilon_{n}}}(0)
to obtain

\|\vartheta_{n}-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}<\varepsilon_{0}/3
,
for all
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
If additionally,
n\in\mathbb{N}
is such that
\|\vartheta-\vartheta_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}<\varepsilon_{0}/3
,
then
\|\vartheta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}<\varepsilon_{0}
.
Since
\varepsilon_{0}>0
was arbitrary, we obtain

\forall\ \varepsilon_{0}>0\ \exists\ n\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }\varepsilon_{n}>0,\text{ such that }\varepsilon\in\overline{B_{\varepsilon_{n}}}(0)\text{ implies }\sup_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|\vartheta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}<\varepsilon_{0}.

To prove (3), we first note that for
m=0
, all claims in this part are easy consequences
of the definitions and of item (2).
Therefore, we can assume
m\in\underline{k+1}
.
Apply Leibniz’s rule to obtain

\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=\sum_{n=0}^{m}\left(\binom{m}{n}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{n}}\left(e^{2\pi i\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle\varepsilon\rangle,\Phi^{-1}(\cdot+\tau)-\Phi^{-1}(\tau)\rangle}\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right).

(6.10)

Moreover, Faa Di Bruno’s formula
[
28
, Corollary 2.10]
—a form of the chain rule for higher derivatives—yields
for
n\in\underline{m}
that

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{n}}\left(e^{2\pi i\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle\varepsilon\rangle,\Phi^{-1}(\cdot+\tau)-\Phi^{-1}(\tau)\rangle}\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=e^{2\pi i\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle\varepsilon\rangle,\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)-\Phi^{-1}(\tau)\rangle}\cdot P_{n,\tau,\varepsilon}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}P_{n,\tau,\varepsilon}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}),

where

\begin{split}P_{n,\tau,\varepsilon}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})&=\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left((2\pi i)^{\ell}\cdot\sum_{\sigma\in(\underline{n-\ell+1})^{\ell}}\left(C_{\sigma}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\frac{\partial^{\sigma_{i}}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\sigma_{i}}}\left\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle\varepsilon\rangle,\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)-\Phi^{-1}(\tau)\right\rangle\right)\right)\\
&=\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left((2\pi i)^{\ell}\cdot\sum_{\sigma\in(\underline{n-\ell+1})^{\ell}}\left(C_{\sigma}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\frac{\partial^{\sigma_{i}}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\sigma_{i}}}\left\langle\varepsilon,A^{-1}(\tau)\langle\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\rangle\right\rangle\right)\right),\end{split}

(6.11)

for suitable constants
C_{\sigma}\geq 0
.
For the second equality, note that
\sigma_{i}\geq 1
for all
i
,
so that the term
\langle A^{-T}(\tau)\langle\varepsilon\rangle,\Phi^{-1}(\tau)\rangle
—which is constant with respect to

\textstyle\Upsilon

—can be ignored.
In fact, the main statement of Faa Di Bruno’s formula is exactly which

C_{\sigma}
are nonzero and what value they attain, see also Lemma
8.6
,
but these details are not required here.
Similar to (
4.27
), we have that

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{\sigma_{i}}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\sigma_{i}}}\left\langle\varepsilon,A^{-1}(\tau)\langle\Phi^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\rangle\right\rangle&=\frac{\partial^{\sigma_{i}-1}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\sigma_{i}-1}}\left\langle\varepsilon,A^{-1}(\tau)A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\langle e_{i}\rangle\right\rangle\\
&=\frac{\partial^{\sigma_{i}-1}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\sigma_{i}-1}}\left(\left[A^{-1}(\tau)A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\tau)\right]^{T}\varepsilon\right)_{i}=\left(\frac{\partial^{\sigma_{i}-1}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\sigma_{i}-1}}\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle\varepsilon\rangle\right)_{i}\,\,,\end{split}

where
\phi_{\tau}=\left[A^{-1}(\tau)A(\cdot+\tau)\right]^{T}
is as in (
4.4
).
By (
4.5
), we can estimate

\left|\left(\frac{\partial^{\sigma_{i}-1}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\sigma_{i}-1}}\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\langle\varepsilon\rangle\right)_{i}\right|\leq\left\|\frac{\partial^{\sigma_{i}-1}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{\sigma_{i}-1}}\phi_{\tau}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right\|\cdot|\varepsilon|\leq v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot|\varepsilon|\quad\text{ and inserting this into \eqref{eq:PMTEest},}

|P_{n,\tau,\varepsilon}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})|\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left((2\pi\cdot v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot|\varepsilon|)^{\ell}\cdot\sum_{\sigma\in(\underline{n-\ell+1})^{\ell}}C_{\sigma}\right)\leq\tilde{C}\cdot|\varepsilon|\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})^{\ell}\cdot|\varepsilon|^{\ell-1}\right),

for a suitably large
\tilde{C}=\tilde{C}(n)>0
.
Since we only consider
n\in\underline{m}
, we can in fact choose the same constant
\tilde{C}

for all values of
n
.
Moreover,
1\leq v_{0}^{\ell}\leq v_{0}^{n}
for all
\ell\leq n
.

By assembling all the pieces and by separating the term
n=0

in (
6.10
), we thus get

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\vartheta\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right|&\leq\sum_{n=1}^{m}\binom{m}{n}\left|(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}P_{n,\tau,\varepsilon})({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot\left(\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right|\\
&=\sum_{n=1}^{m}\binom{m}{n}\left|P_{n,\tau,\varepsilon}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot\left(\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right|\\
&\leq|\varepsilon|\cdot\sum_{n=1}^{m}\left(\left|\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right|\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(\tilde{C}\binom{m}{n}v_{0}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})^{\ell}\cdot|\varepsilon|^{\ell-1}\right)\right)\\
&\leq|\varepsilon|\cdot\sum_{n=1}^{m}\left(\left|\left(v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right)\right|\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\left(\tilde{C}\binom{m}{n}\cdot|\varepsilon|^{\ell-1}\right)\right).\end{split}

Let

0\leq C_{m,\varepsilon}:=\max_{n\in\underline{m}}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\binom{m}{n}\cdot\tilde{C}|\varepsilon|^{\ell-1}\right)<\infty

to obtain the estimate

\left|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\vartheta\right)({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right|\leq C_{m,\varepsilon}\cdot|\varepsilon|\cdot\sum_{n=1}^{m}\left|v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right|.

Since
\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}
is solid,
we conclude

\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\vartheta\right)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\leq C_{m,\varepsilon}\cdot|\varepsilon|\cdot\sum_{n=1}^{m}\left\|v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}<\infty.

(6.12)

Finally, with
C_{m,\varepsilon}\leq C_{m,\varepsilon_{0}\cdot e_{1}}=:C_{m,\varepsilon_{0}}
for
|\varepsilon|\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\vartheta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\vartheta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\vartheta\right)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}+\left\|\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\vartheta\right)-\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\right)\xrightarrow[|\varepsilon|\rightarrow 0]{}0,

as a consequence of part (2),
and (
6.12
).

To prove (
6.9
)
(and thus also
\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}
),
observe
\|\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}f\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}=\|f\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}
for all

f\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
By Equation (
6.12
) the triangle inequality for norms yields

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}(\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\vartheta)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\left(\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\vartheta\right)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}+C_{m,\varepsilon}\cdot|\varepsilon|\cdot\sum_{n=1}^{m}\left\|v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\vartheta\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}+C_{m,\varepsilon}\cdot|\varepsilon|\cdot\sum_{n=1}^{m}\left\|v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\vartheta\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}.

This proves (
6.9
), since
C_{m,\varepsilon}\leq C_{m,\varepsilon_{0}}
as noted before.
∎

We now show that
\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}
uniformly converges to
0
as
\delta\!\to\!\!0
,
for
(y,\omega)\!\in\!\Lambda
and
(z,\eta)\!\in\!(y+\mathbf{P}^{\delta}_{\omega})\times\mathbf{Q}^{\delta}_{\omega}
.
Recall that
(y+\mathbf{P}^{\delta}_{\omega})\times\mathbf{Q}^{\delta}_{\omega}
was introduced in Lemma
5.3

as a simple superset to
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{(y,\omega)}=\bigcup_{V_{\ell,k}\ni(y,\omega)}V_{\ell,k}
,
appearing in the oscillation. The considered notion of convergence is in terms of the

\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}
-norm of certain derivatives of
\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}
.
With Lemma
6.4
, obtaining the desired estimates for
\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}

amounts to little more than an application of the triangle inequality and a somewhat elaborate three-
\varepsilon
-argument.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
q\in[1,\infty)
and let
\tilde{w}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a continuous, submultiplicative weight function.
Furthermore, assume that
\Phi
is a
k
-admissible warping function with control weight
v_{0}
.
If

\theta\in\mathcal{C}^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{ for some }0\leq m\leq k+1,\quad\text{ and }\quad v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\theta\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{ for all }0\leq n\leq m,\ j\in\underline{d},

then

\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}=\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\left(\theta-\sqrt{\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}}\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\Phi(\omega),A^{T}(\Phi(\omega))\langle y-z\rangle}\left(\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\eta)-\Phi(\omega)}\theta\right)\right)\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

(6.13)

for all
(y,\omega),(z,\eta)\in\Lambda
, and
j\in\underline{d}
.
Furthermore, with

F_{j,m}(\delta;\theta,q,\tilde{w}):=\sup_{(y,\omega)\in\Lambda}\,\,\sup_{z\in(y+\mathbf{P}_{\omega}^{\delta}),\eta\in\mathbf{Q}_{\omega}^{\delta}}\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}},

(6.14)

where
\mathbf{Q}_{\omega}^{\delta}
and
\mathbf{P}_{\omega}^{\delta}
are as in Lemma
5.3
,
we have

F_{j,m}(\delta;\theta,q,\tilde{w})<\infty\quad\text{ for all }\delta>0,\qquad\text{ and }\quad F_{j,m}(\delta;\theta,q,\tilde{w})\xrightarrow[\delta\to 0]{}0.

(6.15)

Proof.

Since
v_{0}
and
\tilde{w}
are submultiplicative, so is

v_{0}^{n}\tilde{w}
, and
\mathbf{L}^{q}_{v_{0}^{n}\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is translation-invariant,
see (
3.5
).
Hence, since

\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}\theta\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{v_{0}^{n}\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

0\leq n\leq m
and
i\in\underline{d}
, the same holds for arbitrary translates.
Thus,
Lemma

6.4
(3) shows

\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\theta,\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\mathbf{T}_{\tau_{0}}\theta)\in\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for all
\tau_{0},\tau,\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. This establishes (
6.13
), since

\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}<\infty
.

Fix
\delta>0
and
(y,\omega)\in\Lambda

and
(z,\eta)\in(y+\mathbf{P}_{\omega}^{\delta})\times\mathbf{Q}_{\omega}^{\delta}
.
For brevity, set
\tau:=\Phi(\omega)-\Phi(\eta)
and

\varepsilon:=A^{T}(\Phi(\omega))\langle y-z\rangle
, noting that
\tau\in B_{2\delta}(0)
and

\varepsilon\in A^{T}(\Phi(\omega))\langle\mathbf{P}_{\omega}^{\delta}\rangle=B_{2\delta v_{0}(\delta)}(0)=:B_{\varepsilon_{\delta}}(0).

In particular,
\varepsilon_{\delta}\leq\varepsilon_{\delta_{0}}
, for all
\delta\leq\delta_{0}
,
and
\varepsilon_{\delta}\rightarrow 0
as
\delta\rightarrow 0
.
Recall the definition of
\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}
(given in (
6.5
)),
and apply the triangle inequality twice to obtain the estimate

\begin{split}\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\tilde{\theta}_{(y,\omega),(z,\eta)}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}&\leq\!\left|1\!-\!\sqrt{\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}}\right|\cdot\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\theta\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}+\sqrt{\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}}\cdot\!\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\!\left(\theta\!-\!\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\Phi(\omega),\varepsilon}\theta\right)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\\
&\quad+\sqrt{\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}}\cdot\!\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\Phi(\omega),\varepsilon}(\theta\!-\!\mathbf{T}_{\Phi(\eta)-\Phi(\omega)}\theta)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\!.\end{split}

(6.16)

Next, Lemma
6.4
(3) yields

\displaystyle E_{\delta}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{|\varepsilon|\leq\varepsilon_{\delta}}\,\,\sup_{\omega\in D}\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\!\left(\theta-\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\Phi(\omega),\varepsilon}\theta\right)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}\leq\infty,\ \text{for all}\ \delta>0,\text{ with }E_{\delta}\rightarrow 0\ \text{ as }\delta\rightarrow 0,\text{ and}

(6.17)

\displaystyle F_{\delta}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{|\varepsilon|\leq\varepsilon_{\delta}}\,\,\sup_{\omega\in D}\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\mathbf{\operatorname{E}}_{\Phi(\omega),\varepsilon}(\theta\!-\!\mathbf{T}_{-\tau}\theta)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\theta-\mathbf{T}_{-\tau}\left(\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\theta\right)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}+C_{m,\varepsilon_{\delta_{0}}}\cdot\varepsilon_{\delta}\cdot\sum_{n=1}^{m}\left\|v_{0}^{n}\cdot\frac{\partial^{m-n}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m-n}}(\theta\!-\!\mathbf{T}_{-\tau}\theta)\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}}.

(6.18)

Note that the first term of the right-hand side of (
6.18
) converges to
0

for
\delta\rightarrow 0
, since
|\tau|\leq 2\delta
and translation is continuous
in
\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}
, since
\tilde{w}
is continuous and submultiplicative.
Furthermore, the sum over
n
in the right-hand side of (
6.18
) is finite,
since
\mathbf{L}^{q}_{\tilde{w}}
is translation-invariant and hence,
all summands are finite by assumption.
Therefore,
F_{\delta}
vanishes for
\delta\rightarrow 0
.
In fact, since
|\varepsilon|\leq\varepsilon_{\delta}
and
w
is
v_{0}^{d}
-moderate
with radially increasing
v_{0}
(cf.
Lemma

4.9
),

\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}\leq v_{0}^{d}(\varepsilon_{\delta})
,
which settles the desired convergence of the second and third term
in (
6.16
).

To settle convergence of the first term, we need to show that

\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}\overset{\delta\rightarrow 0}{\rightarrow}1
,
uniformly with respect to
\omega\in D,\eta\in\mathbf{Q}_{\omega}^{\delta}
.
To this end, note that

\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}=\frac{w(\Phi(\eta))+\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{dt}\big|_{t=s}\left[w(\Phi(\eta)+s\tau)\right]~ds}{w(\Phi(\eta))}\leq 1+\frac{\sup_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in B_{2\delta}(\Phi(\eta))}\nabla_{\tau}w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})}{w(\Phi(\eta))},

where
\nabla_{\tau}
denotes the derivative in direction
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
We now use Jacobi’s formula

\frac{d}{dt}\det A(t)=\det A(t)\cdot\mathop{\operatorname{trace}}([A(t)]^{-1}\cdot A^{\prime}(t)),

valid for the derivative of the determinant of any differentiable function

M:I\subset\mathbb{R}\to\mathrm{GL}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

(see
[
70
, Section 8.3, Equation (2)]
),
to obtain

\begin{split}\nabla_{\tau}w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})&=\sum_{j\in\underline{d}}\tau_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}}\det(A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}))=\det(A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}))\cdot\sum_{j\in\underline{d}}\tau_{j}\cdot\mathop{\operatorname{trace}}\left(A^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\frac{\partial}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}}A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right)\\
&=w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot\sum_{j\in\underline{d}}\tau_{j}\cdot\mathop{\operatorname{trace}}\left(\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta_{i}}\bigg|_{\eta=0}A^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})A({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}+\eta)\right)^{T}\right)\\
&=w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot\sum_{j\in\underline{d}}\tau_{j}\cdot\mathop{\operatorname{trace}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta_{i}}\bigg|_{\eta=0}\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(\eta)\right),\end{split}

with
\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}
as in (
4.4
).
Note that
\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}}(0)=\mathop{\operatorname{id}}
for all

{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, so that (
4.5
) yields

\|(\partial_{i}\phi_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}})(0)\|\leq v_{0}(0)
.
Additionally, the trace of a matrix
M\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
can be (coarsely) estimated by

|\mathop{\operatorname{trace}}(M)|\leq d\|M\|
, such that

\left|\nabla_{\tau}w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\right|\leq d\cdot w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot\sum_{j\in\underline{d}}v_{0}(0)\cdot|\tau_{j}|\leq d\cdot w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot\|\tau\|_{1}\cdot v_{0}(0)\leq d^{3/2}\cdot w({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})\cdot|\tau|\cdot v_{0}(0).

Therefore, with
|\tau|\leq 2\delta
and
v_{0}^{d}
-moderateness of
w
,

\left|1-\frac{w(\Phi(\omega))}{w(\Phi(\eta))}\right|\leq|\tau|\cdot d^{3/2}\cdot v_{0}(0)\cdot\max_{r\in[0,1]}\frac{w(\Phi(\eta)+r\tau)}{w(\Phi(\eta))}\leq 2\delta\cdot d^{3/2}\cdot v_{0}^{d}(2\delta)\cdot v_{0}(0)=:C^{\delta}<\infty.

(6.19)

The final estimate is independent of
\omega\in D
, and of
\eta\in\mathbf{Q}_{\omega}^{\delta}
,
and
C^{\delta}\rightarrow 0
as
\delta\rightarrow 0
.
∎

We are now ready to prove
Theorem

6.1
.

6.2
Proof of Theorem
6.1

Recall that, by
Remark

2.17
,
{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}
is continuous.
Using
Proposition

6.3
and Parseval’s formula,
we can rewrite the oscillation at
((y,\omega),(z,\eta))\in\Lambda\times\Lambda
, as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}((y,\omega),(z,\eta))

\displaystyle=\sup_{(z_{0},\eta_{0})\in\mathcal{V}_{(z,\eta)}^{\delta}}\left|\left\langle\widehat{g_{y,\omega}},\widehat{g_{z,\eta}}-\Gamma((z,\eta),(z_{0},\eta_{0}))\cdot\widehat{g_{z_{0},\eta_{0}}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{(z_{0},\eta_{0})\in\mathcal{V}_{(z,\eta)}^{\delta}}\left|K_{\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{(z,\eta),(z_{0},\eta_{0})},\Phi}((y,\omega),(z,\eta))\right|.

(6.20)

Based on (
6.20
),

Lemma

4.7
provides

\begin{split}\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\left|K_{\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{(z,\eta),(z_{0},\eta_{0})},\Phi}((y,\omega),(z,\eta))\right|$\mbox{}\hfil\\
&=\sqrt{\frac{w(\Phi(\eta))}{w(\Phi(\omega))}}\cdot\left|L_{\Phi(\eta)}[\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{(z,\eta),(z_{0},\eta_{0})}](A^{T}(\Phi(\eta))\langle z-y\rangle,\Phi(\omega)-\Phi(\eta))\right|,\end{split}

where
L_{\Phi(\eta)}
is as in (
4.9
).
If we define
\mathcal{L}_{\tau_{0}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}
,
\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, by

\mathcal{L}_{\tau_{0}}(x,\tau):=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\,\sup_{(z_{0},\eta_{0})\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{z,\Phi^{-1}(\tau_{0})}}\left|L_{\tau_{0}}[\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{(z,\Phi^{-1}(\tau_{0})),(z_{0},\eta_{0})}](x,\tau)\right|,

(6.21)

then, for all
(y,\omega),(z,\eta)\in\Lambda
,

{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}((y,\omega),(z,\eta))\leq\sqrt{\frac{w(\Phi(\eta))}{w(\Phi(\omega))}}\mathcal{L}_{\Phi(\eta)}(A^{T}(\Phi(\eta))\langle z-y\rangle,\Phi(\omega)-\Phi(\eta)).

Via a tedious, but straightforward derivation involving several changes of variable
in a manner similar to the proof of
Lemma

4.7
, we obtain in particular that

\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}\leq\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m^{\natural}}}\leq\mathop{\operatorname{ess~sup}}_{\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}M(x,\tau)\mathcal{L}_{\tau_{0}}(x,\tau)~dx~d\tau,

(6.22)

where
M
is defined as in (
4.2
) and we used that

m
is
\Phi
-compatible with the (symmetric) dominating weight

m^{\Phi}(x,\tau)=(1+|x|)^{p}\cdot v_{1}(\tau)
.

By
Lemma

6.5
, with
\tilde{w}\!=\!w_{2}
,
all functions
\tilde{\theta}_{(z,\eta),(z_{0},\eta_{0})}
with
(z,\eta)\!\!\in\!\Lambda
and

(z_{0},\eta_{0})\!\!\in\!\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{z,\eta}\!\subset\!(z+\mathbf{P}^{\delta}_{\eta})\times\mathbf{Q}^{\delta}_{\eta}

satisfy the conditions of
Theorem

4.8
, as does
\theta
.
Hence, for any
z,\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
(z_{0},\eta_{0})\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{z,\Phi^{-1}(\tau_{0})}
,

Theorem

4.8
yields

|L_{\tau_{0}}[\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{(z,\Phi^{-1}(\tau_{0})),(z_{0},\eta_{0})}](x,\tau)|\leq C\cdot C_{\textrm{max}}\cdot(1+|x|)^{-(d+p+1)}\cdot v_{0}^{4d+3p+3}(\tau)\cdot[w_{2}(\tau)]^{-1},

(6.23)

where
C>0
depends only on
d
,
k
and the control weight
v_{0}
, and furthermore

\begin{split}C_{\textrm{max}}=&\,\,C_{\textrm{max}}\left(d+p+1,\theta,\tilde{\theta}_{(z,\Phi^{-1}(\tau_{0})),(z_{0},\eta_{0})}\right)\\
=&\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\underline{d}\\
0\leq m\leq d+p+1\end{subarray}}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\theta\bigg\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}_{w_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\cdot\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\underline{d}\\
0\leq m\leq d+p+1\end{subarray}}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\tilde{\theta}_{(z,\Phi^{-1}(\tau_{0})),(z_{0},\eta_{0})}\bigg\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}_{w_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
({\scriptstyle\text{Lem. \ref{lem:convergence_of_tildetheta}}})\,\leq&\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\underline{d}\\
0\leq m\leq d+p+1\end{subarray}}\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{m}}{\partial{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}_{j}^{m}}\theta\bigg\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}_{w_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\cdot\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\underline{d}\\
0\leq m\leq d+p+1\end{subarray}}F_{j,m}(\delta;\theta,2,w_{2})=:D_{\textrm{max}}^{\delta}<\infty.\end{split}

Note that the estimate
D_{\textrm{max}}^{\delta}
is independent of
\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
,

z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
(z_{0},\eta_{0})\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{z,\Phi^{-1}(\tau_{0})}
,
such that taking
D_{\textrm{max}}^{\delta}
instead of
C_{\textrm{max}}

in (
6.23
) produces a valid upper estimate for
\mathcal{L}_{\tau_{0}}(x,\tau)
.
Moreover, note that
Lemma

6.5
implies

D_{\textrm{max}}^{\delta}\rightarrow 0
as
\delta\rightarrow 0
.

Proving
\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}<\infty
is now analogous
to the proof of
Theorem

4.4
, and
\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m}}\rightarrow 0

as
\delta\rightarrow 0
follows directly from
D_{\textrm{max}}^{\delta}\rightarrow 0
.
∎

7
Coorbit space theory of warped time-frequency systems

We have now developed explicit sufficient conditions that ensure
K_{\theta,\Phi},{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\in\mathcal{B}_{m}
and hence, by Eq. (
2.23
),

\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}^{\delta}}K_{\theta,\Phi}\in\mathcal{B}_{m}
, since
\mathcal{B}_{m}
is solid.
These are the crucial ingredients for applying coorbit theory
in the setting of warped time-frequency representations.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
\Phi
be a
(d+p+1)
-admissible warping function with control weight
v_{0}
,
where
p=0
if
R_{\Phi}=\sup_{\xi\in D}\|\mathrm{D}\Phi(\xi)\|=\infty
and
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
otherwise.
Let furthermore
m_{0}:\Lambda\times\Lambda\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a symmetric weight
that satisfies
1\leq m_{0}(\lambda,\rho)\leq C^{(0)}m_{0}(\lambda,{\nu})m_{0}({\nu},\rho)

for all
\lambda,\rho,{\nu}\in\Lambda
and

m_{0}((y,\xi),(z,\eta))\leq(1+|y-z|)^{p}\cdot v_{1}(\Phi(\xi)-\Phi(\eta)),\text{ for all }y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ and }\xi,\eta\in D,\ \tau,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(7.1)

for some continuous and submultiplicative weight
v_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}

with
v_{1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})=v_{1}(-{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}})
for all
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{$\displaystyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\textstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptstyle\Upsilon$}}{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scriptscriptstyle\Upsilon$}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Then there exist nonzero
\theta\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{v_{0}^{d/2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, such that for any rich,
solid Banach space
Y\hookrightarrow\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\Lambda)

with
\mathcal{B}_{m_{0}}(Y)\hookrightarrow Y
,

1.

\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),Y)
is a well-defined Banach function space.

2.

There is a
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\theta,\Phi,m_{0})>0
independent of
Y
, such that

(g_{y_{\ell,k},\omega_{\ell,k}})_{\ell,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\subset\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi)

is a Banach frame decomposition for
\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),Y)
, whenever
the points
\left((y_{\ell,k},\omega_{\ell,k})\right)_{\ell,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\subset\Lambda

satisfy
(y_{\ell,k},\omega_{\ell,k})\in V^{\delta}_{\ell,k}
, where

\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}^{\delta}=(V_{\ell,k}^{\delta})_{\ell,k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}

is the
\Phi
-induced
\delta
-fine covering and
\delta\leq\delta_{0}
.

In particular, items (1) and (2) above hold for
Y=\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa}(\Lambda)
,
with
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
and any weight
\kappa:\Lambda\rightarrow[1,\infty)
that satisfies

m_{\kappa}\lesssim m_{0}
.

Proof.

By
Propositions

5.2
and
5.4
, the
\Phi
-induced

\delta
-fine covering
\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}^{\delta}
is a topologically admissible,
product-admissible covering that satisfies items (1)-(3) of
2.11

and item (1) of
2.19
.
Moreover, item (6) of
2.11
is satisfied,
by the assumptions of this theorem.

Next, choose
\theta\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, such that
\|\theta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=1

and the assumptions of
Theorem

6.1
are satisfied with
m=m_{v}
defined by

\displaystyle m_{v}((y,\omega),(z,\eta))=\max\left\{\frac{v((y,\omega))}{v((z,\eta))},\frac{v((z,\eta))}{v((y,\omega))}\right\},

with

\displaystyle v((y,\omega)):=m_{0}((y,\omega),(x,\xi))\cdot\max\{w(\Phi(\omega)),[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1}\},

for all
(y,\omega),(z,\eta)\in\Lambda
and some fixed, arbitrary
(x,\xi)\in\Lambda
.
This is always possible, since any function

\theta\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with unit
\mathbf{L}^{2}
-norm
satisfies these assumptions.
In particular, the assumptions of
Theorem

6.1

are also satisfied for
m=m_{0}\leq m_{v}
.
By
Proposition

3.4
, the map
(y,\omega)\mapsto g_{y,\omega}

is continuous and by
Corollary

3.6
, the warped time-frequency system

\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi)
is a tight Parseval frame,
such that item (4) of
2.11
is satisfied.
In particular, by Eq. (
3.7
),

\sup_{(y,\omega)\in\Lambda}\|g_{y,\omega}\|_{2}\leq\|\theta\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}}<\infty
.
Hence, with
w^{c}_{\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}^{\delta}}=\max\{w(\Phi(\omega)),[w(\Phi(\omega))]^{-1}\}

as in
Proposition

5.2
and
u(\lambda):=m_{0}(\lambda,(x,\xi))

with the same choice of
(x,\xi)\in\Lambda
as above, item (5) of
2.11

is satisfied as well.

Moreover, by choice of
\theta
, and with
\Gamma
as in
Theorem

6.1
, we have

\|K_{\theta,\Phi}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m_{v}}}<\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}^{\delta}}K_{\theta,\Phi}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m_{0}}}\leq\|K_{\theta,\Phi}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m_{0}}}+\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m_{0}}}<\infty,

showing that the final item (7) of
2.11
is satisfied.
Hence,
2.11
is fully satisfied and we can apply
Theorem

2.14

to show that
\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),Y)
is a well-defined Banach function space.

Finally, note that
\Gamma
as in
Theorem

6.1
is continuous,
to verify that item (2) of
2.19
is satisfied.
By the same theorem, we can choose
\delta_{0}>0
, such that

\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m_{v}}}\cdot(2\|K_{\Psi}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m_{v}}}+\|{\mathrm{osc}}_{\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\Phi},\Gamma}\|_{\mathcal{B}_{m_{v}}})<1

for all
\delta\leq\delta_{0}
, proving the second assertion.
The proof is completed by observing that the statement about weighted,
mixed-norm Lebesgue spaces is a direct consequence of (
2.14
).
∎

By definition, the coorbit space
\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta,\Phi),Y)
depends
on both the prototype function
\theta
and the warping function
\Phi
.
The dependence on the warping function
\Phi
is an essential consequence
of (sufficiently) different warping functions inducing time-frequency representations
with vastly different properties. Relations between coorbit spaces associated
to different warping functions are studied in the framework of decomposition spaces
[
42
,
19
,
95
]

in a follow-up contribution.
Here, we will show that the dependence on the generating prototype
\theta
can be weakened,
i.e., under certain conditions on
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
,
the coorbit spaces
\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta_{1},\Phi),Y)

and
\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta_{2},\Phi),Y)
are equal,
similar to modulation spaces for the STFT.
Before we do so, however, we show that the mixed kernel associated with
two warped time-frequency systems inherits the membership in
\mathcal{B}_{m}
(or
\mathcal{A}_{m}
)
from the kernels of the individual systems.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
X\in\{\mathcal{A}_{m},\mathcal{B}_{m}\}
, with a symmetric weight
m

satisfying
m(\lambda,\rho)\leq C^{(0)}m(\lambda,{\nu})m({\nu},\rho)
,
for some
C^{(0)}
and all
\lambda,\rho,{\nu}\in\Lambda
.
If
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}\cap\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are nonzero and such that

K_{\theta_{1},\Phi},K_{\theta_{2},\Phi}\in X
, then

K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\Phi}:=K_{\mathcal{G}(\theta_{1},\Phi),\mathcal{G}(\theta_{2},\Phi)}\in X.

(7.2)

Proof.

We first consider the case
\langle\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\rangle\neq 0
.
In that case, the orthogonality relations, Theorem
3.5
,
applied to the kernel
K_{\theta_{1},\Phi}\cdot K_{\theta_{2},\Phi}
yield,
for all
(y,\omega),(z,\eta)\in\Lambda
,

\begin{split}K_{\theta_{1},\Phi}\cdot K_{\theta_{2},\Phi}((y,\omega),(z,\eta))&=\int_{\Lambda}K_{\theta_{1},\Phi}((y,\omega),(x,\xi))K_{\theta_{2},\Phi}((x,\xi),(z,\eta))~d(x,\xi)\\
&=\int_{\Lambda}\overline{\langle g^{(1)}_{y,\omega},g^{(1)}_{x,\xi}\rangle}\langle g^{(2)}_{z,\eta},g^{(2)}_{x,\xi}\rangle~d(x,\xi)\\
({\scriptstyle{\text{Def.\ of }V_{\bullet,\Phi}}})&=\int_{\Lambda}V_{\theta_{2},\Phi}g^{(2)}_{z,\eta}(x,\xi)\overline{V_{\theta_{1},\Phi}g^{(1)}_{y,\omega}(x,\xi)}~d(x,\xi)=\langle V_{\theta_{2},\Phi}g^{(2)}_{z,\eta},V_{\theta_{1},\Phi}g^{(1)}_{y,\omega}\rangle\\
({\scriptstyle{\text{orth.~rel.}}})&=\langle g^{(2)}_{z,\eta},g^{(1)}_{y,\omega}\rangle\langle\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\rangle=\langle\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\rangle\cdot K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\Phi}((y,\omega),(z,\eta)).\end{split}

Since, under the conditions on
m
,
\mathcal{A}_{m},\mathcal{B}_{m}
are algebrae, this establishes (
7.2
).

If
\langle\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\rangle=0
,
then we need an auxiliary function
\theta_{3}
,
which may be any function in
\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}\cap\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

K_{\theta_{3},\Phi}\in X
and that is neither orthogonal to

\theta_{1}
nor to
\theta_{2}
.
For example,
\theta_{3}
could satisfy the conditions of
Theorem.
4.4
.
By the first part of the proof, we obtain

(K_{\theta_{1},\Phi}\cdot K_{\theta_{3},\Phi})\cdot(K_{\theta_{3},\Phi}\cdot K_{\theta_{2},\Phi})=\langle\theta_{1},\theta_{3}\rangle\overline{\langle\theta_{2},\theta_{3}\rangle}\cdot\ K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{3},\Phi}\cdot K_{\theta_{3},\theta_{2},\Phi}\in X.

Now, apply the argument in the first part of the proof again to obtain that

K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\Phi}=C^{-1}(K_{\theta_{1},\Phi}\cdot K_{\theta_{3},\Phi})\cdot(K_{\theta_{3},\Phi}\cdot K_{\theta_{2},\Phi})\qquad\text{with}\qquad C=\|\theta_{3}\|^{2}\langle\theta_{1},\theta_{3}\rangle\overline{\langle\theta_{2},\theta_{3}\rangle}.\qed

Remark 7.3
.

If
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
satisfy the conditions of Theorem
4.4
,
then the assumptions of Lemma
7.2
can be verified by applying that theorem.
However, since Theorem
4.4
only provides
sufficient
conditions,
there might be
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
with
K_{\theta_{1},\Phi},K_{\theta_{2},\Phi}\in\mathcal{B}_{m}

that do not satisfy those conditions, for which Lemma
7.2
remains valid.

Theorem 7.4
.

Assume that
\Phi
,
m_{0}
and both
\theta_{1}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}
and
\theta_{2}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}_{\sqrt{w_{0}}}
jointly satisfy
the conditions of
Theorem

6.1
.

Then, for any rich, solid Banach space

Y\hookrightarrow\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\Lambda)

with
\mathcal{B}_{m_{0}}(Y)\hookrightarrow Y
, we have

\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta_{1},\Phi),Y)=\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta_{2},\Phi),Y).

In particular, the statement holds for
Y=\mathbf{L}^{p,q}_{\kappa}(\mu)
,
with
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
and any weight
\kappa:\Lambda\rightarrow[1,\infty)

that satisfies
m_{\kappa}\lesssim m_{0}
.

Proof.

The same derivations as in the proof of
Theorem

7.1
show that

2.11
and
2.19
are fully satisfied and
consequently, by
Theorem

2.14
,
\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta_{1},\Phi),Y)
and

\operatorname{Co}(\mathcal{G}(\theta_{2},\Phi),Y)
are well-defined Banach spaces.
By
Lemma

7.2
, the mixed kernel
K_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}

is contained in
\mathcal{B}_{m_{v}}\subset\mathcal{B}_{m_{0}}
,
with
v
as in the proof of
Theorem

7.1
.
Hence, we can apply
Proposition

2.15
to obtain the desired result.
The statement about weighted, mixed-norm Lebesgue spaces is, once more,
a direct consequence of (
2.14
).
∎

8
Radial warping

In this section, we consider warped time-frequency representations
for which the warping of frequency space depends only
on the modulus in the frequency domain, i.e., we study maps of the form

\Phi_{\varrho}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},\xi\mapsto\xi/|\xi|\cdot\varrho(|\xi|)\,,

which we call the
radial warping function
associated
to the
radial component

\varrho:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
.
More precisely, we will provide conditions on the radial component
\varrho

which ensure that
\Phi_{\varrho}
is a (
k
-admissible) warping function, as
introduced in Definitions
3.1
and

4.2
.
In particular, we will see that if
\varrho
is a strictly increasing

\mathcal{C}^{k+1}
diffeomorphism which is also linear on a neighborhood of the origin,
then
\Phi_{\varrho}
is a
\mathcal{C}^{k+1}
diffeomorphism, with inverse

\Phi_{\varrho}^{-1}=\Phi_{\varrho^{-1}}
.
Finally, under additional “moderateness assumptions” on the derivatives
of
\varrho^{-1}
, we will show that the diffeomorphism
\Phi_{\varrho}

is a
k
-admissible warping function.
These claims will be established in
Section
8.1
.

Section

8.2
is concerned with circumventing the somewhat unnatural restriction
that
\varrho
is linear in a neighborhood of the origin.
Using the so-called
slow-start construction
,
one can associate to a “sufficiently well-behaved” function
\varsigma:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)

a
k
-admissible radial component
\varrho:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
,
which equals
\varsigma
outside an arbitrarily small neighborhood of the origin.

Finally, we discuss several examples of radial warping functions
in Section
8.3
.

8.1
General properties of radial warping functions

To enable a more compact notation, we will from now on denote by

\varrho_{\ast}:=\varrho^{-1}
the inverse of a bijection
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. A function
\varrho:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is called a

k
-admissible radial component with control weight

v:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
if the following hold:

1.

\varrho
is a strictly increasing
\mathcal{C}^{k+1}
-diffeomorphism with inverse

\varrho_{\ast}=\varrho^{-1}
.

2.

\varrho
is antisymmetric, that is,
\varrho(-\xi)=-\varrho(\xi)
for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
.
In particular,
\varrho(0)=0
.

3.

There are
\varepsilon>0
and
c>0
with
